Hi, yeah, thanks. Um, I guess the main thing is I just get pulled off track by every little sound or thought. Like, I’ll be sitting there trying to work on a job application—which, by the way, those online portals they make you use now are the absolute worst. Yeah, it's definitely not just the job stuff. It happens with pretty much everything, even stuff I actually want to do. Like, I’ve been trying to fix up this old dirt bike I bought off Craigslist—the guy said it just needed a new carburetor, but honestly, I think the whole engine block is shot. He totally ripped me off, but whatever. Anyway, I'll try to watch a YouTube video on how to rebuild it, or I'll try to read the service manual, and I'll get maybe two or three minutes in before I realize I'm just staring blankly at the screen. I end up having to rewind the video like five times because absolutely none of it stuck in my head. 

It’s just like when I try to watch movies now. I tried watching that new sci-fi movie everyone is talking about, the one with the huge spaceships that look like giant floating rocks? The special effects are crazy good, but the plot makes zero sense. Like, why would aliens travel across the entire galaxy just to steal our water? They could literally just mine ice from comets. It’s such a lazy trope. My brother kept telling me to shut up because I was pointing out all the plot holes, but I couldn't help it. 

Wait, what was the question again? Oh, right, other areas of my life. Yeah, it happens all the time. I just zone out and have to start over, whether it's reading a manual or watching a video or whatever.

Well, no, I mean... it's always been there, I guess. Like, even back in elementary school, my teachers were constantly moving my desk around. I was always talking out of turn or forgetting my homework. I'd just rush through worksheets and make all these dumb, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done. 

It reminds me of Mrs. Gable in third grade. She was this super strict older lady who smelled like peppermint and mothballs. She had this whole system where if you talked, she'd move your clip down on this giant traffic light on the chalkboard. I swear my clip lived on red. One time, she caught me drawing this massive battle scene on my desk with a dry-erase marker. I was trying to draw the Battle of Hoth from Star Wars, you know, with the AT-AT walkers? I actually got really good at drawing the snowspeeders, but the proportions were always weird because the walkers are supposed to be huge. I tried to explain to her that it was dry-erase and would wipe right off, but she made me scrub all the desks in the classroom during recess anyway. Which actually wasn't that bad because the sponge smelled like lemons, and I hate the smell of the mulch they use on the playground. It always gets stuck in your shoes and then you track it into the house, and my mom would lose her mind...

Wait, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, school. Yeah, so the attention stuff has been an issue since I was a little kid, honestly. The soccer injury just made it worse because I lost my routine.

Yeah, oh man, all the time. It's actually kind of embarrassing. Like, if I'm sitting there talking to someone, my leg is just going a mile a minute, just bouncing up and down. Or I'll be clicking a pen over and over, or jingling my keys in my pocket, and I don't even realize I'm doing it until someone points it out. 

My buddy Dave actually threw a pen across the room once because I wouldn't stop clicking it while we were trying to figure out our fantasy football draft. Which, by the way, my team was an absolute disaster this year. I drafted this running back in the first round because all the analysts said he was gonna have a breakout season, right? But then his offensive line was basically made of Swiss cheese. I mean, the quarterback was running for his life on every single play. It's like they didn't even practice blocking during training camp. And don't even get me started on the coaching staff. They kept calling these ridiculous screen passes on third and long, like the defense wasn't going to see that coming from a mile away. I ended up coming in last place in the league, which means I have to do the punishment this year. We have this whole thing where the loser has to spend 24 hours in a Waffle House, but you can knock an hour off for every waffle you eat. I've been trying to figure out the math on it, like, is it better to just pace yourself and eat one an hour, or do you try to eat ten right away and make yourself sick? I feel like the syrup is what really gets you...

Wait, sorry, I totally lost track there. What were we talking about? Oh, right, the fidgeting thing. Yeah, I'm always messing with my hands or bouncing my leg. It's just really hard to sit completely still.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, so... the fidgeting and the focus stuff, it was definitely there in middle and high school. I mean, I was always the kid tapping his pencil or staring out the window. But honestly, it wasn't this bad back then. It really only got to this level of... I don't know, just completely taking over my life... about a year and ten months ago. 

That's when I had the soccer injury. I tore my ACL playing in this Sunday league. We were actually playing against this team from the next town over, and they were playing so dirty. Like, slide tackling from behind, throwing elbows on corner kicks. The ref was this older guy who looked like he could barely see past the midfield line, so he wasn't calling anything. I was going up for a header off a cross—and it was a beautiful cross, perfectly placed right at the penalty spot—and this massive defender just completely undercuts me. I landed so awkwardly, and I heard this loud pop. It sounded like someone snapping a thick branch in half. The worst part was, I had just bought these brand new cleats, too. They were the bright neon green ones that all the pros were wearing at the World Cup. I spent way too much money on them, and I only got to wear them for like three games before they had to cut them off me in the emergency room. I was so mad about the cleats, I almost didn't even care about my knee at first. And the physical therapy was a nightmare. The therapist was this guy named Chad who made me do these resistance band exercises that felt like torture...

Wait, sorry. The timeline, right. Yeah, so it's been this disruptive for about a year and ten months. Before the injury, I had soccer and a whole routine that kind of kept me in check, but once that was gone, everything just completely fell apart.

I honestly couldn't tell you. I never really looked at my report cards. My mom was always the one who grabbed the mail before I even got home.

I'd just rush through worksheets and make all these dumb, careless mistakes because I just wanted to be done. \n\nIt reminds me of Mrs. Gable in third grade. I was trying to draw the Battle of Hoth from Star Wars, you know, with the AT-AT walkers? I actually got really good at drawing the snowspeeders, but the proportions were always weird because the walkers are supposed to be huge. Yeah, I graduated. I got my diploma and everything. The graduation ceremony was actually held at the big arena downtown, the one where the hockey team plays. It was freezing in there, like they forgot to turn off the ice rink cooling system or something. We were all shivering under those thin polyester gowns. My gown was supposed to be royal blue, but it looked more like a faded purple under the arena lights. My buddy Eric sneaked a beach ball in under his gown, and we kept bouncing it around during the principal's speech. The principal was this guy Mr. Henderson, who always wore these really loud, patterned bow ties. Like, polka dots and stripes together. He was going on and on about the future and soaring like eagles, and then the beach ball hit him right on the side of the head. It was hilarious, but they actually held Eric's diploma for a week because of it. 

Anyway, what were you asking? Oh, repeating grades. No, I didn't have to repeat any grades. I just moved up each year until I was done.

Yeah, I heard that a lot. Like, constantly. "Not living up to your potential" was basically the theme of my childhood. Even way back in elementary school, I remember teachers were always getting frustrated with me. They were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever sat next to me.

She had this huge jar of those strawberry candies on her desk, the ones with the wrappers that look like actual strawberries? I don't know who actually buys those, they just seem to magically appear in older people's houses. But anyway, she had this jar, and I would just sit there and count them instead of doing my work. I was always forgetting to bring my homework in, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets as fast as humanly possible so I could go play, and they'd be full of careless mistakes. 

Speaking of rushing, that reminds me of this time I tried to build a go-kart out of some old lawnmower parts I found in the neighbor's trash. I didn't really have a plan, I just kind of started bolting things to this piece of plywood. I used these tiny little wheels off a broken wagon, and the steering was just a rope tied to the front axle. I dragged it up to the top of Miller's Hill—which is way steeper than it looks, by the way—and just sat on it and let go. The rope instantly snapped, and I just went flying straight into Mrs. Higgins' prize-winning rose bushes. I got so many thorns stuck in my arm, my mom had to use tweezers to pull them out for like an hour. She was so mad, not even about the go-kart, but because I ruined my favorite pair of jeans. They were those really baggy ones that were popular back then, with the huge pockets...

Wait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, my potential. Yeah, teachers always said I was smart but just wouldn't apply myself. They said I was always rushing and making mistakes and talking too much.

I spent way too much money on them, and I only got to wear them for like three games before they had to cut them off me in the emergency room. I was so mad about the cleats, I almost didn't even care about my knee at first. And the physical therapy was a nightmare. Yeah, so it's been this disruptive for about a year and ten months. Before the injury, I had soccer and a whole routine that kind of kept me in check, but once that was gone, everything just completely fell apart.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you were always fidgety and had trouble focusing back in middle and high school, but I want to understand this better. During those years—say, when you were 13 to 18—were you able to get through school okay despite the fidgeting? Like, were you passing your classes, or were you struggling academically back then too?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know, honestly. I guess I got through it? I don't really remember my exact grades or anything. \n\nLike, I do remember my sophomore year biology class with Mr. Harrison. He had this massive fish tank in the back of the room, and I would just stare at the filter bubbling for, like, half the period. One time, he made us do this huge partner project on biomes, and I got paired with this guy, Kevin. Kevin was super into building his own computers, which was actually really cool. We were supposed to be making a diorama of a rainforest, but we ended up spending the entire weekend at his house trying to overclock his CPU so we could run this new game on max settings. He had this crazy cooling system with literal neon blue liquid pumping through these tubes inside the case. It looked like something out of a sci-fi movie. We actually pushed it way too hard and the whole thing just shut down and started smelling like burning plastic, and his mom came down and started yelling at us because she thought we were starting a fire in the basement. She made us go outside, so we ended up just wandering over to 7-Eleven to get Slurpees. I always get the cherry and coke mixed together, but the cherry side of the machine was broken so I had to get blue raspberry, which is fine, but it just turns your mouth completely blue for like two days...\n\nWait, sorry, my bad. What were you asking me? About passing my classes? Yeah, I'm not really sure how I did, to be honest. I just kind of sat in the back and existed.\n\nDoctor: When you look back at your actual grades from high school—like your report cards or transcripts—do you remember if you were getting mostly A's and B's, or were you getting C's, D's, and failing grades?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. I never really looked at my report cards. My mom was always the one who grabbed the mail before I even got home. \n\nWe had this mailman, Gary, and he drove one of those old postal jeeps where the steering wheel is on the right side instead of the left. I always thought that was the coolest thing when I was a kid. Actually, a couple of years ago, I got really obsessed with trying to import a right-hand drive car from Japan. Like an old Nissan Skyline. Have you ever looked into that? It's a massive headache. You have to deal with all these customs forms and emissions testing, and there's this 25-year rule where the car has to be a certain age before they even let it into the country. I spent, like, three straight weeks just watching YouTube videos about Japanese car auctions and trying to calculate shipping costs from Yokohama to Baltimore. I even made this huge spreadsheet color-coding the different auction grades, but then my laptop charger broke and I kind of just abandoned the whole project. The replacement charger for that specific laptop was going to be like eighty bucks, which is a total ripoff for just a piece of wire and a plastic brick. I ended up trying to splice the wires together myself with some electrical tape, which, uh, definitely did not work and actually sparked a little bit... \n\nWait, what was the question? Something about the mail? Oh, right, my grades. Yeah, no, I really don't remember what the actual letters on the paper were.\n\nDoctor: Did you graduate from high school, and do you remember if you were able to pass your classes and move forward each year, or did you have to repeat any grades or drop out?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I graduated. I got my diploma and everything. The graduation ceremony was actually held at the big arena downtown, the one where the hockey team plays. It was freezing in there, like they forgot to turn off the ice rink cooling system or something. We were all shivering under those thin polyester gowns. My gown was supposed to be royal blue, but it looked more like a faded purple under the arena lights. My buddy Eric sneaked a beach ball in under his gown, and we kept bouncing it around during the principal's speech. The principal was this guy Mr. Henderson, who always wore these really loud, patterned bow ties. Like, polka dots and stripes together. He was going on and on about the future and soaring like eagles, and then the beach ball hit him right on the side of the head. It was hilarious, but they actually held Eric's diploma for a week because of it. \n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, repeating grades. No, I didn't have to repeat any grades. I just moved up each year until I was done.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you graduated high school and moved forward each year. But thinking about your overall performance in school—not just whether you passed—did teachers or your parents ever express concern that you were underperforming compared to what they thought you were capable of? Like, did anyone ever say you weren't living up to your potential?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I heard that a lot. Like, constantly. \"Not living up to your potential\" was basically the theme of my childhood. Even way back in elementary school, I remember teachers were always getting frustrated with me. They were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever sat next to me. \n\nActually, in third grade, my teacher Mrs. Gable moved my desk so it was literally attached to hers at the front of the room. She had this huge jar of those strawberry candies on her desk, the ones with the wrappers that look like actual strawberries? I don't know who actually buys those, they just seem to magically appear in older people's houses. But anyway, she had this jar, and I would just sit there and count them instead of doing my work. I was always forgetting to bring my homework in, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets as fast as humanly possible so I could go play, and they'd be full of careless mistakes. \n\nSpeaking of rushing, that reminds me of this time I tried to build a go-kart out of some old lawnmower parts I found in the neighbor's trash. I didn't really have a plan, I just kind of started bolting things to this piece of plywood. I used these tiny little wheels off a broken wagon, and the steering was just a rope tied to the front axle. I dragged it up to the top of Miller's Hill—which is way steeper than it looks, by the way—and just sat on it and let go. The rope instantly snapped, and I just went flying straight into Mrs. Higgins' prize-winning rose bushes. I got so many thorns stuck in my arm, my mom had to use tweezers to pull them out for like an hour. She was so mad, not even about the go-kart, but because I ruined my favorite pair of jeans. They were those really baggy ones that were popular back then, with the huge pockets...\n\nWait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, my potential. Yeah, teachers always said I was smart but just wouldn't apply myself.   },

Well, right now I'm actually unemployed. I've been trying to get into this automotive technician program, because like I said, I'm really good with cars. Well, except for the go-kart thing, but real cars I understand. The hands-on stuff just makes sense to my brain. 

But trying to actually get into the program has been a nightmare. I keep missing the deadlines for the online training modules you have to do before they even let you near a shop. The website they use is so clunky, it looks like it was built in 1998. It has that weird gray background with the blue hyperlinks? And every time you click something, it opens in a new window instead of a new tab. I hate that. It clutters up my whole screen. I actually spent like two hours trying to find a browser extension that forces everything to open in a tab, but then I went down this rabbit hole reading reviews for different ad blockers, and by the time I looked at the clock, it was 3 AM. I ended up downloading this one that changes all the ads to pictures of cats, which is funny for about five minutes until you're trying to read a serious article and there's just a giant orange tabby staring at you. 

Anyway, because I keep getting distracted and missing the deadlines, I've had to redo the whole application process a couple of times. So yeah, I'm just kind of stuck right now, not really moving forward with the technician thing, even though I know I could do the actual work if they'd just let me in the garage.

The rope instantly snapped, and I just went flying straight into Mrs. Higgins' prize-winning rose bushes. I got so many thorns stuck in my arm, my mom had to use tweezers to pull them out for like an hour. She was so mad, not even about the go-kart, but because I ruined my favorite pair of jeans. Yeah, teachers always said I was smart but just wouldn't apply myself. It was hilarious, but they actually held Eric's diploma for a week because of it. \n\nAnyway, what were you asking? Oh, repeating grades. No, I didn't have to repeat any grades. I just moved up each year until I was done.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you graduated high school and moved forward each year. But thinking about your overall performance in school—not just whether you passed—did teachers or your parents ever express concern that you were underperforming compared to what they thought you were capable of? Like, did anyone ever say you weren't living up to your potential?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I heard that a lot. Like, constantly. \"Not living up to your potential\" was basically the theme of my childhood. Even way back in elementary school, I remember teachers were always getting frustrated with me. They were constantly moving my seat because I was always talking out of turn or bothering whoever sat next to me. \n\nActually, in third grade, my teacher Mrs. Gable moved my desk so it was literally attached to hers at the front of the room. She had this huge jar of those strawberry candies on her desk, the ones with the wrappers that look like actual strawberries? I don't know who actually buys those, they just seem to magically appear in older people's houses. But anyway, she had this jar, and I would just sit there and count them instead of doing my work. I was always forgetting to bring my homework in, or if I did do it, I'd just rush through the worksheets as fast as humanly possible so I could go play, and they'd be full of careless mistakes. \n\nSpeaking of rushing, that reminds me of this time I tried to build a go-kart out of some old lawnmower parts I found in the neighbor's trash. I didn't really have a plan, I just kind of started bolting things to this piece of plywood. I used these tiny little wheels off a broken wagon, and the steering was just a rope tied to the front axle. I dragged it up to the top of Miller's Hill—which is way steeper than it looks, by the way—and just sat on it and let go. The rope instantly snapped, and I just went flying straight into Mrs. Higgins' prize-winning rose bushes. I got so many thorns stuck in my arm, my mom had to use tweezers to pull them out for like an hour. She was so mad, not even about the go-kart, but because I ruined my favorite pair of jeans. They were those really baggy ones that were popular back then, with the huge pockets...\n\nWait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, my potential. Yeah, teachers always said I was smart but just wouldn't apply myself. They said I was always rushing and making mistakes and talking too much.\n\nDoctor: Right now, because of all these attention and fidgeting problems you've been having, how is it affecting your ability to work or maintain a job? Are you currently employed, and if so, are you having trouble keeping up with your responsibilities at work?\n\nPatient: Well, right now I'm actually unemployed. I've been trying to get into this automotive technician program, because like I said, I'm really good with cars. Well, except for the go-kart thing, but real cars I understand. The hands-on stuff just makes sense to my brain. \n\nBut trying to actually get into the program has been a nightmare. I keep missing the deadlines for the online training modules you have to do before they even let you near a shop. The website they use is so clunky, it looks like it was built in 1998. It has that weird gray background with the blue hyperlinks? And every time you click something, it opens in a new window instead of a new tab. I hate that. It clutters up my whole screen. I actually spent like two hours trying to find a browser extension that forces everything to open in a tab, but then I went down this rabbit hole reading reviews for different ad blockers, and by the time I looked at the clock, it was 3 AM. I ended up downloading this one that changes all the ads to pictures of cats, which is funny for about five minutes until you're trying to read a serious article and there's just a giant orange tabby staring at you. \n\nAnyway, because I keep getting distracted and missing the deadlines, I've had to redo the whole application process a couple of times. Like, who blows leaves at 7:30 on a Sunday morning? And it's not even autumn! There are barely any leaves on the ground. He's just blowing dust around. I watched him out the window for like twenty minutes the other day. He has this really specific pattern he follows, back and forth across the driveway, but he always misses this one corner near the mailbox. It drives me crazy. I almost went out there to tell him, but I was in the middle of making a sandwich. I tried making one of those fancy grilled cheeses I saw on a cooking show, with like three different kinds of cheese and some caramelized onions. But I got distracted by the leaf blower guy and totally burned the bread. It was practically charcoal. I had to scrape it off with a butter knife over the sink, which makes this awful scratching sound... \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know if there's a specific time I can just focus perfectly. It's just kind of always happening."
